tmp_path = WindowsPath('[USER_HOME]/Github/s19_app-b103-g3-repro-residual/head/main/tmp/test_r_a2l_read_1_crlf_cr_mixe0')

    def test_r_a2l_read_1_crlf_cr_mixed_read_as_lf(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
        """R-A2L-READ.1/1..3: CRLF, lone CR and mixed ends each read as LF (35/35)."""
        for path in _tracked_a2l():
            text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
            base = parse_a2l_file(path)
            base_tags = {t["name"]: _six_fields(t) for t in base["tags"]}
            for variant in ("crlf", "cr", "mixed"):
                if variant == "crlf":
                    body = text.replace("\n", "\r\n")
                elif variant == "cr":
                    body = text.replace("\n", "\r")
                else:
                    body = "".join(
                        ln + ("\r\n" if i % 3 == 0 else "\r" if i % 3 == 1 else "\n")
                        for i, ln in enumerate(text.split("\n"))
                    )
                data = _parse(tmp_path, body, name="line.a2l")
                assert len(data["tags"]) == len(base["tags"]), path
                for t in data["tags"]:
                    assert _six_fields(t) == base_tags[t["name"]], (path, t["name"])
>               assert data["errors"] == base["errors"], path

_________________ test_cov_read_1_line_end_variants_equal_lf __________________

tmp_path = WindowsPath('[USER_HOME]/Github/s19_app-b103-g3-repro-residual/head/main/tmp/test_cov_read_1_line_end_varia0')

    def test_cov_read_1_line_end_variants_equal_lf(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
        """R-A2L-READ.1/1..3: tags, sections (line numbers included) and errors
        equal the LF reading, not just the six tag fields."""
        for path in _tracked_a2l():
            text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
            base = _parse(tmp_path, text, name="lf.a2l")
            for variant in ("crlf", "cr", "mixed"):
                if variant == "crlf":
                    body = text.replace("\n", "\r\n")
                elif variant == "cr":
                    body = text.replace("\n", "\r")
                else:
                    parts = text.split("\n")
                    mixed = []
                    for i, line in enumerate(parts):
                        mixed.append(line)
                        if i + 1 < len(parts):
                            mixed.append("\r" if not line else ("\r\n", "\r", "\n")[i % 3])
                    body = "".join(mixed)
                data = _parse(tmp_path, body, name="line.a2l")
>               _assert_same_section_tree(data["sections"], base["sections"], (str(path), variant))

    def _assert_same_section_tree(left: List[dict], right: List[dict], context: tuple) -> None:
        """Assert equal section trees and identify any differing scalar field."""
        assert len(left) == len(right), context
        for index, (sec, bsec) in enumerate(zip(left, right)):
            route = context + (index,)
            differing = {
                key: (value, bsec.get(key))

    def test_cov_read_1_cr_cr_lf_full_equality(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
        """R-A2L-READ.1/4: every tag and error equal the LF reading, and every
        section line is 2*k-1 of the LF file's line."""
        text = _body(meas("X", " ECU_ADDRESS 0x1234"))
        base = _parse(tmp_path, text)
        data = _parse(tmp_path, text.replace("\n", "\r\r\n"))
        assert data["errors"] == base["errors"]
        assert data["tags"] == base["tags"]
        assert len(data["sections"]) == len(base["sections"])
        pairs = list(zip(data["sections"], base["sections"]))
        while pairs:
            sec, bsec = pairs.pop()

____________ test_at_b103_i11_2_commented_old_copy_changes_nothing ____________

tmp_path = WindowsPath('[USER_HOME]/Github/s19_app-b103-g3-repro-residual/head/main/tmp/test_at_b103_i11_2_commented_o0')

    def test_at_b103_i11_2_commented_old_copy_changes_nothing(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
        """AT-B103-I11-2 (RP-B28) through the app: exactly one A2L Explorer row X
        at 0x1234, no ``A2L_DUPLICATE_SYMBOL`` issue on the Issues screen, and a
        Workspace A2L summary that names one tag X at 0x1234 (never 0x9999)."""
        from textual.widgets import DataTable, Static

        from s19_app.tui.app import S19TuiApp
        from s19_app.tui.changes.io import emit_s19_from_mem_map
        from s19_app.tui.issues_view import GroupedIssuesPanel, IssueRow

        from tests.support import b100_projects as projects
        from tests.support.b100_workers import settle_workers
        from tests.support.timing import wait_until

        image = tmp_path / "img.s19"
        image.write_text(
            emit_s19_from_mem_map({0x1234: 0xAA}, [(0x1234, 0x1235)]),
            encoding="ascii",
        )
        a2l = tmp_path / "rp_b28.a2l"
        a2l.write_text(
            _body(
                "/*\n" + meas("X", " ECU_ADDRESS 0x9999") + "\n*/\n"
                + meas("X", " ECU_ADDRESS 0x1234")
            ),
            encoding="utf-8",
        )

        def _strip_glyph(cell: str) -> str:
            for glyph in ("\u2713 ", "\xb7 "):
                if cell.startswith(glyph):
                    return cell[len(glyph):]
            return cell

        def _painted(widget) -> str:
            rendered = widget.render()
            return getattr(rendered, "plain", str(rendered))

        async def scenario(app: S19TuiApp, pilot, log) -> None:
            # Load the image first, so the later A2L attach runs the real
            # cross-artifact validation engine (the duplicate-symbol source).
            app.load_selected_file(image)
            await wait_until(
                pilot,
                lambda: app.current_file is not None
                and app.current_file.path.name == image.name,
                f"the image load of {image.name}",
            )
            await settle_workers(pilot)

            # Drive the app's actual A2L attach action.
            app.load_a2l_from_path(a2l)
            await settle_workers(pilot)
            await wait_until(
                pilot,
                lambda: app.current_a2l_path is not None,
                "the A2L attach did not land",
            )

            # A2L Explorer: exactly one row X, at 0x1234.
            app.action_show_screen("a2l")
            for _ in range(6):
                await pilot.pause()
            table = app.query_one("#a2l_tags_list", DataTable)
            rows = [table.get_row_at(i) for i in range(table.row_count)]
            names = [_strip_glyph(str(row[0])) for row in rows]
            assert names == ["X"], f"expected exactly one X row, got {names!r}"
            assert str(rows[0][1]) == "0x00001234", (
                f"expected X at 0x00001234, got {str(rows[0][1])!r}"
            )

            # Issues screen: no A2L_DUPLICATE_SYMBOL issue for X.
            app.action_show_screen("issues")
            for _ in range(6):
                await pilot.pause()
            panel = app.query_one("#validation_issues_groups", GroupedIssuesPanel)
            duplicates = [
                row.issue.code
                for row in panel.query(IssueRow)
                if row.issue.code == "A2L_DUPLICATE_SYMBOL"
            ]
            assert duplicates == [], f"unexpected duplicate-symbol issues: {duplicates!r}"

            # Workspace A2L summary reports one tag X at 0x1234, never 0x9999.
            app.action_show_screen("workspace")
            for _ in range(6):
                await pilot.pause()
            summary = _painted(app.query_one("#a2l_view", Static))
            assert "A2L Tags:" in summary
            assert "- MEASUREMENT X: 0x00001234" in summary
            assert "0x00009999" not in summary

>       projects.run_app(tmp_path / "app", scenario, size=(160, 50))

    def test_tc_b100_51_every_baseline_bar_site_maps_to_a_seam_event() -> None:
        """LLR-176.2 (A12-28): the 24 baseline ``set_progress`` sites in 12
        functions each map to one begin, step or end of the action table; the
        AST of the app package holds, per function, at least as many seam calls of
        each (event, key, step) as the baseline sites it replaces (24 in all).

        Why: a site left on the raw bar escapes the indicator's rules (stale
        100, no end). Negative control: the report step removed \u2192 the census
        names ``_start_generate_report_worker`` step 2.
        RED (base, P12a-1): 24 sites, no seam call."""
        methods = _merged_methods()
        missing = []
        total = 0
        for function, event, key, step, count in CENSUS:
            total += count
            node = methods.get(function)
            found = 0 if node is None else _seam_events(node).count((event, key, step))
            if found < count:
                missing.append((function, event, key, step, f"{found}/{count}"))
        assert total == 24
>       assert missing == [], missing

    def test_tc_b97_32_patch_help_names_every_label(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
        """TC-B97-32 (LLR-148.6, Patch arm, D20): the ``patch`` help body holds one
        ``label � effect`` line, effect non-empty, for every button, tab chip,
        input and select label the screen renders over its driven states
        (Inspector, JSON and Checks tabs; the save-back prompt and the
        before/after report offered), every key it binds and every glyph of
        ``PATCH_GLYPHS``. The floor is derived over ``PE_IDS_9C7BEAF`` by the type
        tuple Button � Input � Select and equals 26; the count of covered control
        WIDGETS is at least that floor; the one ``Edit JSON`` line names both tabs.

        RED on main (P-21): 6 of 19 labels named. Mutation: a body line without
        `` � effect``; coverage from one tab only; the ``TextArea`` put back in
        the tuple (derived 27 \u2260 26).
        """
        from textual.widgets import Button, Input as _Input, Select as _Select

        from s19_app.tui import screens_directionb as sdb
        from tests.b97_fixtures import PE_IDS_9C7BEAF, calib_hotfix, load_change_file, open_patch_editor

        async def _drive():
            app = S19TuiApp(base_dir=tmp_path)
            async with app.run_test(size=(118, 54)) as pilot:
                panel = await open_patch_editor(app, pilot)
                screen = app.query_one("#screen_patch")
                derived = {
                    i for i in PE_IDS_9C7BEAF
                    if isinstance(app.query_one(f"#{i}"), (Button, _Input, _Select))
                }
                await load_change_file(app, pilot, panel, calib_hotfix(tmp_path))
                seen = {}

                def harvest():
                    for widget in screen.query("*"):
                        if not isinstance(widget, (Button, _Input, _Select)):
                            continue
                        if not widget.display or widget.region.width == 0:
                            continue
                        if any(not a.display for a in widget.ancestors if hasattr(a, "display")):
                            continue
                        seen[widget] = _control_label(screen, widget)

                for tab in PATCH_TABS:
                    if panel.query(f"#{tab}"):
                        panel.query_one(f"#{tab}").press()
                        await settle(pilot, 3)
                    harvest()
                if panel.query("#patch_tab_json"):
                    panel.query_one("#patch_tab_json").press()
                    await settle(pilot, 2)
                panel.query_one("#patch_doc_apply_button").press()
                await settle(pilot, 6)
                harvest()
                panel.query_one("#patch_saveback_confirm_button").press()
                await settle(pilot, 8)
                harvest()
                in_panel = {w: label for w, label in seen.items() if panel in w.ancestors}
                return derived, in_panel, set(seen.values()), window_keys_text(app, "patch")

        derived, in_panel, labels, keys_text = asyncio.run(_drive())
        from s19_app.tui import screens_directionb as sdb

        lines = _help_lines("patch", keys_text)
        # Inc-8 (D27): ^z / ^y are lines of the generated keys block, keyed by the
        # key bar's display (`^z`), no longer the hand-typed `ctrl+z`.
        keys = {"^z", "^y", "space", "enter", "?"}
        glyphs = set(getattr(sdb, "PATCH_GLYPHS", {}) or {"(no PATCH_GLYPHS)"})
        assert len(derived) == 26, sorted(derived)
        missing = sorted((labels | keys | glyphs) - set(lines))

    ) -> None:
        """HLR-167's acceptance scene, by keys and clicks: load ``case_01``

                    "the Save project dialog",
                )

                # name, type it, and wait for its painted summary before Save.

tmp_path = WindowsPath('[USER_HOME]/Github/s19_app-b103-g3-repro-residual/head/main/tmp/test_at_b97_14_help_chip_opens0')
screen_key = 'patch'

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("screen_key", ["patch", "check_editor", "flow", "flow_execution"])
    def test_at_b97_14_help_chip_opens_a_body_covering_the_screen(tmp_path: Path, screen_key: str) -> None:
        """AT-B97-14 (US-138 / HLR-148, black-box): after each screen is driven
        through its states, its ``?`` chip � clicked, then focused and pressed
        with ``enter`` � opens the help modal; the modal body, read as painted,
        parsed into ``label � effect`` lines covers every control label the
        screen painted over those states (inputs included), with the control
        guard 26 / 12 / 21 / 6.

        RED on main (P-21): 6/19 � 0/6 � 2/13 � 0/4 covered.
        """
        from textual.widgets import Button, Input as _Input, Select as _Select

        from tests.b97_fixtures import fx_project
        from tests.b97_probes import painted_rows

        screen_ids = {"patch": "screen_patch", "check_editor": "screen_check_editor", "flow": "screen_flow", "flow_execution": "screen_flow_execution"}

        async def _drive():
            app = S19TuiApp(base_dir=tmp_path)
            async with app.run_test(size=(118, 54)) as pilot:
                await settle(pilot)
                await fx_project(app)
                screen = app.query_one(f"#{screen_ids[screen_key]}")
                labels = {}

                def harvest():
                    for widget in screen.query("*"):
                        if not isinstance(widget, (Button, _Input, _Select)):
                            continue
                        if not widget.display or widget.region.width == 0:
                            continue
                        if any(not a.display for a in widget.ancestors if hasattr(a, "display")):
                            continue
                        labels.setdefault(widget, set()).update(_fx_label(screen, widget))

                await _drive_states(app, pilot, screen_key, harvest)
                bodies = []
                chip_id = f"window_help_{screen_key}"
                for how in ("click", "enter"):
                    chip = app.query_one(f"#{chip_id}")
                    if how == "click":
                        await pilot.click(f"#{chip_id}")
                    else:
                        chip.focus()
                        await _poll(pilot, lambda: app.focused is chip and not chip.has_class("-active"), 120)
                        await pilot.press("enter")
                    opened = await _poll(pilot, lambda: type(app.screen).__name__ == "WindowHelpScreen", 120)
                    if opened:
                        await settle(pilot, 2)
                        modal_text = "\n".join(
                            str(w.render()) for w in app.screen.query("Static") if w.display
                        )
                        bodies.append(modal_text)
                        app.screen.dismiss()
                        await _poll(pilot, lambda: type(app.screen).__name__ != "WindowHelpScreen")
                return labels, bodies

        labels, bodies = asyncio.run(_drive())
        assert len(bodies) == 2, "the ? chip opens the help by click and by enter"
        lines = {}
        for raw in bodies[0].split("\n"):
            text = raw.strip()
            text = text.split(". ", 1)[1] if text[:1].isdigit() and ". " in text else text
            if " � " in text:
                label, effect = text.split(" � ", 1)
                lines[label.strip()] = effect.strip()
        controls = {label for names in labels.values() for label in names if label}
        covered = [w for w, names in labels.items() if names and names <= set(lines)]
        # D20: the guard counts control WIDGETS whose labels the body covers.
        assert len(covered) >= HELP_GUARD[screen_key], (screen_key, len(covered), sorted(controls))
        missing = sorted(controls - set(lines))
>       assert missing == [], f"{screen_key}: painted labels with no help line: {missing}"

_____________ test_tc_b100_44_project_load_asks_iff_a_flag_is_set _____________

tmp_path = WindowsPath('[USER_HOME]/Github/s19_app-b103-g3-repro-residual/head/main/tmp/test_tc_b100_44_project_load_a0')

    def test_tc_b100_44_project_load_asks_iff_a_flag_is_set(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
        """LLR-174.1b, HLR-174a: project P open and nothing unsaved \u2192 a project
        load of Q asks 0 questions and opens Q; a patch plus a Patch Editor
        entry (through the panel's inputs) \u2192 Save project \u2192 a project load asks
        0; with D only, W only and F only \u2192 1 question each (5 of 5).

        Why: a project load replaces the image and resets the Patch Editor, so
        any of the three unsaved flags must be asked about, and an open project
        alone must not be (the ``pb_A`` prototype asked whenever a project was
        open).
        Negative control: the ``pb_A`` rule \u2192 the first arm asks \u2192 RED;
        dropping F or W \u2192 that arm loads silently \u2192 RED. RED (base): the
        single-flag arms ask nothing."""
        scene = _scene(tmp_path)

        async def scenario(app, pilot, log):
            out = {}
            await _open_project(pilot, app, "P")
            spy = PushSpy(app)
            app._handle_load_dialog(LC("project", "Q"))
            await ld.wait_for(pilot, lambda: app.current_project == "Q" or is_question(app.screen), "Q or the question")
            await _settle(pilot, 6)
            out["open, nothing unsaved"] = (spy.questions(), app.current_project)
            if is_question(app.screen):
                await _answer(pilot, app, "n")
            else:
                await _close_result_panel(pilot, app)
            await _patch(pilot, app)
            await pilot.press("6")
            await ld.wait_for(pilot, lambda: len(app.query("#patch_entry_add_button")) > 0, "the Patch Editor")
            await _settle(pilot, 4)
            assert await pilot.click("#patch_entry_address_input")
            await pilot.press("ctrl+a", "delete", *"0x80000010")
            assert await pilot.click("#patch_entry_bytes_input")
            await pilot.press("ctrl+a", "delete", *"AB")
            assert await pilot.click("#patch_entry_add_button")
            await ld.wait_for(pilot, lambda: _panel(app)._dirty, "the panel flag")
            messages = await projects.save_project(pilot, app, log, "S1")
            assert any(m.startswith("Saved project to") for m in messages), messages
            assert not _D(app) and not _panel(app)._dirty, "setup: Save project left a flag set"
            mark = spy.questions()
            app._handle_load_dialog(LC("project", "P"))
            await ld.wait_for(pilot, lambda: app.current_project == "P" or is_question(app.screen), "P or the question")
            await _settle(pilot, 6)
            out["saved"] = (spy.questions() - mark, app.current_project)
            if is_question(app.screen):
                await _answer(pilot, app, "n")
            else:
                await _close_result_panel(pilot, app)
            for flag in ("D", "W", "F"):
                if flag == "D":
                    await _patch(pilot, app)
                elif flag == "W":
                    await _set_w(pilot, app)
                else:
                    await _set_f(pilot, app)
                mark = spy.questions()
                app._handle_load_dialog(LC("project", "Q"))
                await _wait_question(pilot, app)
                out[flag] = spy.questions() - mark
                await _answer(pilot, app, "n")
                # back to nothing unsaved for the next single-flag arm
                if flag == "D":
                    app._change_service.mark_saved(app.current_file.mem_map)
                elif flag == "W":
                    _panel(app).mark_saved()
                else:
                    _flow(app).mark_saved("flow")
                await _settle(pilot, 2)
            return out

    async def _assert_card_painted_then_safe(
        app, pilot, projected: str, *, scroll: bool = False
    ) -> None:
        """Positive-first (R-TUI-042a.7.1): prove ``projected`` � the value's
        control-free projection � is actually painted (scrolling to the end with an
        asserted nonzero effective scroll first when it lives below the card's

        screen output holds none of the four hostile introducers."""

            _assert_card_lines_clean(full)
            _assert_no_introducers(screen)

    def test_r_crc_config_001_11_9_deep_value_gives_the_reason_not_a_raise() -> None:

tests\test_b103_inc12_tag_table.py:1297: AssertionError
_____ test_r_crc_config_001_11_10_operations_execute_survives_deep_value ______

tmp_path = WindowsPath('[USER_HOME]/Github/s19_app-b103-g3-repro-residual/head/main/tmp/test_r_crc_config_001_11_10_op0')

    def test_r_crc_config_001_11_10_operations_execute_survives_deep_value(
        tmp_path: Path,
    ) -> None:
        """E5 F1, through keys: a config whose `init` nests just under the JSON
        reader's limit is loaded and executed in the Operations dialog. The app
        stays up and shows the malformed-config reason for every depth tried.

        The reader's limit inside the dialog depends on the handler's stack, so
        the test searches it by bisection between a depth the reader accepts and
        one it refuses: the search ends on the first depth the number-field path
        does not answer, which on the defect is the first depth whose echo
        raises (the app exits there)."""
        config_path = tmp_path / "deep.json"
        field_status = f"{OperationsScreen.NO_CONFIG_TEXT}\n{_DEEP_INIT_REASON}"
        nesting_status = f"{OperationsScreen.NO_CONFIG_TEXT}\n{_NESTING_REASON}"

        async def _drive() -> dict[int, str]:
            s19_path = tmp_path / "k.s19"
            s19_path.write_text(_kat_image_s19(), encoding="utf-8")
            app = S19TuiApp(base_dir=tmp_path)
            statuses: dict[int, str] = {}
            async with app.run_test() as pilot:
                await pilot.pause()
                await _load_file(app, pilot, s19_path)
                screen = await _open_crc_screen_by_keys(app, pilot)
                await _focus_by_tab(app, pilot, "operation_config_path")
                await pilot.press(*config_path.name)

                async def probe(depth: int) -> str:
                    text = _deep_init_text(depth)
                    config_path.write_text(text, encoding="utf-8")
                    await _focus_by_tab(app, pilot, "operation_config_load")
                    await pilot.press("enter")
                    await _flush(pilot)
                    assert screen.query_one("#operation_config", TextArea).text == text
                    statuses[depth] = await _execute_by_keys(app, pilot, screen)
                    return statuses[depth]

                low, high = 500, 1000
                assert await probe(low) == field_status
                assert await probe(high) == nesting_status
                while high - low > 1:
                    middle = (low + high) // 2
                    if await probe(middle) == field_status:
                        low = middle
                    else:
                        high = middle
                assert app.is_running
            return statuses

    def test_at_b78_32_app_bindings_block_is_untouched(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
        """AT-B78-32 (PIN) -- Lane 1 does not disturb the App ``BINDINGS`` block.

        RE-AUTHORED at batch-79 Phase 0, and the reason is the point of the node.

        The batch-78 spec realizes this as *"a zero-line ``git diff`` over
        ``app.py:1338-1375``"*. Executed against the tree, that range STOPS
        MID-BLOCK: the cited end line falls on the ``crc_designer`` screen binding,
        and **eight** entries sit BELOW it and outside the range --
        ``plus`` / ``minus`` context paging, ``comma`` / ``period`` hex paging,
        ``pagedown`` / ``pageup`` from batch-31 AC-3, and ``ctrl+z`` / ``ctrl+y``
        from batch-40 S2. An entry added or removed among those eight leaves the
        range-based predicate GREEN, so the guard would report "untouched" for a
        block that was touched. It fails in the safe-looking direction.

        \u26a0\ufe0f **That sentence said "the ``page_prev_context`` entry" and "six" until the
        SIXTH merge gate measured it.** Both were wrong, and the second followed from
        the first: ``page_prev_context`` is two lines LOWER than the cited boundary,
        so believing the boundary sat there silently dropped ``plus``/``minus`` from
        the inventory. The figure came in from Phase 0's premise table and was
        carried through two rewrites of this very paragraph without being
        re-executed -- including one whose stated purpose was removing false
        references from it. *A figure inherited inside a sentence you are editing is
        still an inherited figure.* The paragraph's THESIS is unaffected: the range
        does stop mid-block, entries do escape the predicate, and the node's
        assertions never depended on the count.

        It is not drift: at ``f6ff1d3`` the block ALREADY ran past the cited end and
        batch-78 added zero ``Binding(`` lines. The range was wrong when it was
        written and survived three Phase-2 rounds -- because a line range looks like
        a measurement. A code comment in ``screens_directionb.py`` carried the
        CORRECT extent, so the two artifacts disagreed and the code was right.

        **batch-79 Inc-11: this docstring cites no line number as a POINTER, and it
        took three commits to get there.** Numbers still appear below, and they are
        NARRATION -- the record of which ranges were claimed and how each was wrong.
        Read none of them as an address. (Two earlier drafts got this wrong in
        opposite directions: the first claimed "EVERY line number is now gone" while
        five remained, and the fifth gate then noted that one of the survivors,
        ``1339-1393``, happens to be the block's CURRENT extent -- accurate today,
        which is exactly what makes narration read as a pointer. The wording above
        is scoped to POINTERS for that reason.)
        The first pass cited
        ``screens_directionb.py:6712`` for that comment; the branch pushed it to
        ``:6822``. The second pass removed that one and left the block's own range
        ``1338-1392`` in place -- and the very next commit added an import at the
        top of ``app.py`` and shifted the block to ``1339-1393``. The range that had
        been "corrected" was **also wrong on arrival**.

        So the docstring arguing that a line range is "one insertion away from
        failing the same silent way" demonstrated the claim three times on itself.
        Everything is cited by FILE and DESCRIPTION now; a symbol reference that cannot
        go stale is the whole point of the node below.

        A corrected range would not fix this, and this batch proved it twice: any
        edit ABOVE the block shifts it, and both "corrected" ranges were invalidated
        within days -- one of them wrong the moment it was written. This
        resolves the block from the CLASS instead, which no line movement can blind,
        and pins two independent projections of it.
        """

        # projection kept only `key`, so **re-pointing `slash` or `g` to a different
        # action � precisely what Lane 1 does at Inc-9 � was invisible too.**
        #
        # The spec's line-range predicate would have caught every one of those. The

        # strict improvement the Inc-6 record claimed: its counterfactual was an

____________ test_tc_b100_12_cancelled_question_rehomes_the_focus _____________

tmp_path = WindowsPath('[USER_HOME]/Github/s19_app-b103-g3-repro-residual/head/main/tmp/test_tc_b100_12_cancelled_ques0')

    def test_tc_b100_12_cancelled_question_rehomes_the_focus(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
        """LLR-166.1 Inc 10 arm (C08, �C.2, corrected by orchestrator ruling 7):
        after a cancelled question the focus follows ``_rehome_focus_after_load``
        � a visible, healthy focus is kept. Patch window with the focus on the
        visible ``#patch_entry_address_input`` and D set; ``ctrl+l``, a typed
        data file, ``enter`` \u2192 the question \u2192 ``n`` \u2192 the re-home rule ran once
        and the focus is still on ``patch_entry_address_input``. Workspace with
        the focus on ``#files_list`` and D set; a pick of ``x.s19`` \u2192 the
        question \u2192 ``escape`` \u2192 the re-home rule ran once and the focus is still
        on ``files_list`` (2 of 2).

        Why: after a cancelled question the engineer must keep working where
        they were; LLR-166.1 keeps a visible input in the active window and
        moves only a stale focus.
        Negative control: the cancel branch skips ``_rehome_focus_after_load`` \u2192
        the re-home count is 0 \u2192 RED. A cancel that moves a healthy input onto
        the window target \u2192 the first arm lands on ``patch_editor_panel`` \u2192
        RED. RED (base): no question."""
        scene = _scene(tmp_path)

        def spy_rehome(app, calls: list) -> None:
            real = app._rehome_focus_after_load

            def spy():
                calls.append(1)
                return real()

            app._rehome_focus_after_load = spy

        async def patch_window(app, pilot, log):
            await _load(pilot, app, scene.src / "fw.s19")
            await _patch(pilot, app)
            await pilot.press("6")
            await ld.wait_for(pilot, lambda: len(app.query("#patch_entry_address_input")) > 0, "the Patch Editor")
            await _settle(pilot, 4)
            assert await pilot.click("#patch_entry_address_input")
            assert _focused_id(app) == "patch_entry_address_input"
            await pilot.press("ctrl+l")
            await ld.wait_for_dialog(pilot, app)
            await ld.type_into_path(pilot, app, str(scene.src / "b.s19"))
            await pilot.press("enter")
            await _wait_question(pilot, app)
            calls: list = []
            spy_rehome(app, calls)
            await _answer(pilot, app, "n")
            await _settle(pilot, 6)
            return len(calls), _focused_id(app)

        async def workspace(app, pilot, log):
            await _load(pilot, app, scene.src / "fw.s19")
            await _patch(pilot, app)
            await _pick_in_files_list(pilot, app, "x.s19")

    def test_tc_b100_70_project_load_asks_only_while_the_document_is_unsaved(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
        """LLR-167.8 Inc 10 arm (C31, �C.3): an entry added through the panel,

        questions; an entry added again and a refused Save project (empty name,
        the flag stays set) \u2192 a project load asks 1 question naming ``Patch
        Editor entries`` (2 of 2).

        Why: Save project is the way to make Patch Editor work safe; the
        question must believe it, and must not believe a refused save.
        Negative control: Save project skips the panel's ``mark_saved`` \u2192 the
        first arm asks \u2192 RED. RED (base): the second arm asks nothing."""

            assert await pilot.click("#patch_entry_bytes_input")
            await pilot.press("ctrl+a", "delete", *"DE AD")
            assert await pilot.click("#patch_entry_add_button")
            await ld.wait_for(pilot, lambda: _panel(app)._dirty, "the panel flag")

        async def scenario(app, pilot, log):

            assert not _panel(app)._dirty, "setup: Save project left the flag set"
            spy = PushSpy(app)
            app._handle_load_dialog(LC("project", "Q"))
            await ld.wait_for(pilot, lambda: app.current_project == "Q" or is_question(app.screen), "Q or the question")
            await _settle(pilot, 6)

                await _close_result_panel(pilot, app)
            await add_entry(pilot, app, "0x80000010")

tmp_path = WindowsPath('[USER_HOME]/Github/s19_app-b103-g3-repro-residual/head/main/tmp/test_at_b100_15_load_over_unsa0')

    def test_at_b100_15_load_over_unsaved_work_asks_one_question_per_route(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
        """AT-B100-15 (HLR-174, HLR-174a, HLR-174b, HLR-174c, HLR-174d), by keys
        and widget ids only, in a scratch work area at 118x54:

          - question + keep halves: Load ``fw.s19``; a patch added and applied
            in the Patch Editor; Load ``b.s19`` \u2192 one question titled
            ``Replace fw.s19?`` naming the image, the focus on Cancel; ``enter``
            keeps the patched image (the Loaded panel still names ``fw.s19``,
            the patched byte still in the session image). A Workarea Files pick
            of ``x.s19`` asks the same; ``escape`` keeps it.
          - replace half: Load ``b.s19`` again, ``y`` \u2192 ``b.s19`` loads.
          - after-save half: Apply on ``b.s19`` \u2192 Load ``fw.s19`` asks (``n``);
            Save project ``PA`` \u2192 the same Load asks nothing and loads.
          - ``v`` route: open ``PV``, patch, ``v`` to ``firmware_b`` \u2192 the
            question naming ``firmware_b.s19``; ``n`` keeps ``firmware_a``.
          - items half: Flow Builder edits plus the patched image, then a
            project pick \u2192 one question naming the image and ``flow flow``.

        The panel's ``v`` is TC-B100-72's forced-state arm (ruling Q5).
        Declared: the hex view does not repaint the applied bytes on the base
        either (probe in the tester notes), so the kept patch is read from the
        session image and the painted Loaded panel, not the hex view.

        Negative control: the base tree \u2192 Load over the patched image loads
        with no question (P-17) \u2192 RED at the first question."""
        from textual.widgets import Label

        from s19_app.tui.screens import SaveProjectScreen

        scene = _scene(tmp_path)

        def status(app) -> str:
            return str(app.query_one("#log_line_4", Label).render())

        def loaded_names(app) -> str:
            panel = app.screen_stack[0].query_one("#loaded_panel")
            return " ".join(str(getattr(c.render(), "plain", c.render())) for c in panel.query(".loaded-detail"))

        async def load_by_keys(pilot, app, path: Path) -> None:
            await pilot.press("l")
            await ld.wait_for_dialog(pilot, app)
            await ld.type_into_path(pilot, app, str(path))
            assert await pilot.click("#load_ok"), "click missed #load_ok"
            await ld.wait_for_dialog_closed(pilot, app)

        async def add_and_apply(pilot, app, add: bool = True) -> None:
            await pilot.press("6")
            await ld.wait_for(pilot, lambda: len(app.query("#patch_entry_add_button")) > 0, "the Patch Editor")
            await _settle(pilot, 4)
            if add:
                # A second add keeps the first add's text, and ``ctrl+a`` moves to
                # the start rather than selecting it: clear both inputs first
                # (the ``test_b100_document.py`` helper's pattern).
                from textual.widgets import Input

                app.query_one("#patch_entry_address_input", Input).value = ""
                app.query_one("#patch_entry_bytes_input", Input).value = ""
                assert await pilot.click("#patch_entry_address_input")
                await pilot.press("ctrl+a", "delete", *"0x8000007C")
                assert await pilot.click("#patch_entry_bytes_input")
                await pilot.press("ctrl+a", "delete", *"DE AD BE EF")
                assert await pilot.click("#patch_entry_add_button")
                await ld.wait_for(pilot, lambda: "entry added" in status(app), "the entry added")
            assert await pilot.click("#patch_doc_apply_button")
            await ld.wait_for(pilot, lambda: "1 applied" in status(app), "the apply")
            await _settle(pilot, 4)

        async def scenario(app, pilot, log):
            spy = PushSpy(app)
            out = {}
            # Load fw.s19 and patch it by keys.
            await load_by_keys(pilot, app, scene.src / "fw.s19")
            await ld.wait_for(pilot, lambda: app.current_file is not None, "the load of fw.s19")
            await add_and_apply(pilot, app)
            # Question + keep (Load route, enter).
            await load_by_keys(pilot, app, scene.src / "b.s19")
            await _wait_question(pilot, app)
            out["load question"] = (q_title(app), q_body(app), _focused_id(app))
            await _answer(pilot, app, "enter")
            await _settle(pilot, 6)
            out["kept"] = (
                Path(app.current_file.path).name,
                app.current_file.mem_map.get(PATCH_ADDRESS),
                "fw.s19" in loaded_names(app),
            )
            # Workarea Files pick (escape).
            await _pick_in_files_list(pilot, app, "x.s19")
            await _wait_question(pilot, app)
            out["workarea question"] = q_body(app)
            await _answer(pilot, app, "escape")
            await _settle(pilot, 6)
            out["kept after workarea"] = Path(app.current_file.path).name
            # Replace (y).
            await load_by_keys(pilot, app, scene.src / "b.s19")
            await _wait_question(pilot, app)
            await pilot.press("y")
            await ld.wait_for(pilot, lambda: Path(app.current_file.path).name == "b.s19", "the load of b.s19")
            await _settle(pilot, 6)
            out["replaced"] = ("b.s19" in loaded_names(app), is_question(app.screen))
            # After-save: asks before the save, not after.
            await add_and_apply(pilot, app, add=False)
            await load_by_keys(pilot, app, scene.src / "fw.s19")
            await _wait_question(pilot, app)
            await _answer(pilot, app, "n")
            await pilot.press("ctrl+s")
            await ld.wait_for(pilot, lambda: isinstance(app.screen, SaveProjectScreen), "the Save project dialog")
            assert await pilot.click("#project_name")
            # Inc 11 re-pin (census D / F, LLR-175.3h / .4b): clear the prefilled
            # name, type it, and wait for its painted summary before Save.
            await sd.type_and_await_summary(pilot, app, "PA")
            assert await pilot.click("#save_ok")
            await ld.wait_for(pilot, lambda: "Saved project" in status(app), "the save")
            mark = spy.questions()
            await load_by_keys(pilot, app, scene.src / "fw.s19")
            await ld.wait_for(
                pilot,
                lambda: is_question(app.screen) or Path(app.current_file.path).name == "fw.s19",
                "the load of fw.s19 after the save",
            )
            await _settle(pilot, 6)
            out["after save"] = (spy.questions() - mark, Path(app.current_file.path).name)
            if is_question(app.screen):
                await _answer(pilot, app, "n")
            # v route.
            await pilot.press("1")
            await _settle(pilot, 4)
            await ld.open_project_through_dialog(pilot, app, "PV")
            await projects.dismiss_load_result_panel(pilot, app)
            await add_and_apply(pilot, app)
            await pilot.press("1")
            await _settle(pilot, 4)
            await _switch_variant_by_keys(pilot, app)
            await _wait_question(pilot, app)
            out["v question"] = (q_title(app), q_body(app))
            await _answer(pilot, app, "n")
            await _settle(pilot, 6)
            out["v kept"] = (app._variant_set.active_id, Path(app.current_file.path).name)
            # Items: Flow Builder edits + the patched image, then a project pick.
            await pilot.press("8")
            await _settle(pilot, 6)
            assert await pilot.click("#flow_ref")
            await pilot.press(*"fw.s19")
            assert await pilot.click("#flow_add")
            await ld.wait_for(
                pilot, lambda: "sev-warning" in app.query_one("#flow_name_strip").classes, "the flow marked unsaved"
            )
            await pilot.press("1")
            await _settle(pilot, 4)
            mark = spy.questions()
            await ld.open_project_through_dialog(pilot, app, "PA")
            await _wait_question(pilot, app)
            out["items question"] = (spy.questions() - mark, q_body(app))
            await _answer(pilot, app, "n")
            return out

tmp_path = WindowsPath('[USER_HOME]/Github/s19_app-b103-g3-repro-residual/head/main/tmp/test_at_b78_03_the_palette_act0')

    def test_at_b78_03_the_palette_action_set_matches_the_frozen_artifact(
        tmp_path: Path,
    ) -> None:
        """AT-B78-03 (GATE) -- the action set equals the Inc-0 ARTIFACT, not the producer.

        \u26a0\ufe0f **This node was provably INERT before Inc-0 froze the artifact, and the
        reason is worth carrying.** `CommandBar` is constructed as
        `CommandBar(self._build_palette_entries())`, so an implementation comparing
        the rendered palette against `_build_palette_entries()` compares a producer
        with ITSELF: executed, the predicate stayed GREEN at `36 == 36` with a whole
        `Binding` removed from `S19TuiApp.BINDINGS`.

        What breaks the circularity is the TEMPORAL FREEZE -- a set captured before
        the change and read back from disk. So this reads
        `tests/goldens/batch78/at-b78-03-palette-actions.json` and **never
        regenerates it**; a run that rebuilds it has restored the tautology.
        """
        import json

        from s19_app.tui.command_bar import CommandBar

        frozen = json.loads(
            Path("tests/goldens/batch78/at-b78-03-palette-actions.json").read_text(
                encoding="utf-8"
            )
        )
        expected = frozen if isinstance(frozen, list) else frozen.get("actions", frozen)

        async def _drive():
            app = S19TuiApp(base_dir=tmp_path)
            async with app.run_test(size=(120, 30)) as pilot:
                await pilot.pause()
                bar = app.query_one(CommandBar)
                await pilot.press("ctrl+k")
                await pilot.pause()
                rows = list(bar.query_one("#palette_list").children)
                return [item.data for item in rows]

        observed = asyncio.run(_drive())

        # Union of both lanes' amendments to the frozen artifact (RC-1 rebase #4):
        # batch-95 change 4 (LLR-135.6, ruling Q6/7a) hand-appended ONE action �
        # "open_split_join_export", the palette-first Split / Join / Export screen
        # entry � so the freeze now carries the 37 Inc-0 rows plus that one
        # reviewed amendment (a REGENERATION would restore the tautology this
        # node exists to break). batch-94's two non-digit rail bindings are NOT
        # in the freeze � each is pinned as a named live-only delta below
        # (Inc-001's Check Editor `=`; Inc-003's Flow Execution `]`, US-007).
        assert len(expected) == 38, (
            f"the frozen artifact must still carry the 37 Inc-0 actions plus the "
            f"one batch-95 amendment; it has {len(expected)} -- if this fails "
            f"the artifact was REGENERATED, which restores the tautology"
        )
        live_deltas = ["show_screen('check_editor')", "show_screen('flow_execution')"]
        for added in live_deltas:
            assert added in observed, (
                f"a non-digit rail binding must add exactly one palette action "
                f"({added!r}); observed={observed!r}"
            )
        observed_pre_change = [
            a for a in observed if a not in live_deltas
        ]
        assert len(observed_pre_change) == len(observed) - len(live_deltas), (
            "each live-only delta must appear exactly once (no duplicates)"
        )
>       assert observed_pre_change == list(expected), (
            f"the pre-change palette action set must equal the Inc-0 freeze.\n"
            f"missing: {sorted(set(expected) - set(observed_pre_change))}\n"
            f"unexpected: {sorted(set(observed_pre_change) - set(expected))}"
        )

    def test_tc_b100_53_reset_runs_after_the_worker_and_before_the_first_install(
        tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
    ) -> None:
        """LLR-177.1g, LLR-165.1 (P1-29): a call-order spy over the end of the
        worker step (``_prepare_project_load``), ``_reset_project_session`` and
        ``_apply_loaded_file`` \u2192 ``["worker_end", "reset@main", "install",
        "install"]`` for ``P2``; a ``PermissionError`` forced into
        ``read_project_manifest`` \u2192 0 resets and the session kept.

        Why: a reset before the worker leaves an empty session when the worker
        fails (LLR-177.2); a reset after the first install wipes it.
        Negative control: a reset in ``_handle_load_project`` before the
        worker \u2192 the failure arm leaves ``current_file is None`` \u2192 RED; the
        base (no worker step) \u2192 no ``worker_end`` \u2192 RED."""
        base = tmp_path / "app"
        trio(W(base) / "P2")
        trio(W(base) / "PF")
        events: list = []
        main_box = [threading.get_ident()]

        def _end(*_a, **_k):
            events.append("worker_end" if threading.get_ident() != main_box[0] else "worker_end@main")

        def _reset(*_a, **_k):
            events.append("reset@main" if threading.get_ident() == main_box[0] else "reset@worker")

        Spy(monkeypatch, S19TuiApp, "_prepare_project_load", after=_end)
        Spy(monkeypatch, S19TuiApp, "_reset_project_session", before=_reset)
        Spy(monkeypatch, S19TuiApp, "_apply_loaded_file", before=lambda *_a, **_k: events.append("install"))
        real_manifest = app_module.deps.read_project_manifest

        def _manifest(project_dir, *a, **k):
            if Path(project_dir).name == "PF":
                raise PermissionError("synthetic")
            return real_manifest(project_dir, *a, **k)

        monkeypatch.setattr(app_module.deps, "read_project_manifest", _manifest)

        async def scenario(app, pilot, log):
            await open_trio_project(app, pilot, "P2")
            ok = list(events)
            events.clear()
            try:
                app._handle_load_project("PF")
            except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 � the base raises in the request frame
                pass
            await settle_workers(pilot)
            return ok, list(events), app.current_project, app.current_file is not None

        ok, failed, project, has_image = run(base, scenario)

        carries a real delay; on the synchronous base tree the predicate already
        holds at round one and no time passes."""

tmp_path = WindowsPath('[USER_HOME]/Github/s19_app-b103-g3-repro-residual/head/main/tmp/test_at_b99_05_save_project_af0')

    def test_at_b99_05_save_project_after_same_name_save_back(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
        """Load -> patch -> save back as firmware.s19 -> Save project: the app is
        running, the project folder holds the image and the status names it.
        Driven entirely by keys and clicks through the real Load file dialog,
        the Patch Editor (rail key ``6``) and the real ``SaveProjectScreen``
        (review finding F5) � inputs are clicked and typed, buttons are clicked,
        and every wait observes the painted status line (``#log_line_4``) or the
        painted screen; assertions read the painted status and the files on disk.

        RED at HEAD (P-15): the save-back deletes the work copy and the next Save
        project exits the app with code 1.
        """
        from textual.widgets import Label

        image = tmp_path / "firmware.s19"
        image.write_text(_emit_two(0x11, 0x00), encoding="ascii")

        async def _drive() -> dict:
            app = S19TuiApp(base_dir=tmp_path)
            outcomes: dict = {}
            # 260x54: the Patch Editor's docked save-back row sits at the foot
            # of the JSON pane and is only clickable/painted at the taller
            # viewport (the b97 ATs and AT-B99-02 drive the panel at 120x54);
            # the extra width keeps the "Saved project to �" success line
            # unpainted-clipped in the four-line log strip (review finding
            # LOW-2).
            async with app.run_test(size=(260, 54)) as pilot:
                await pilot.pause()
                # 1. Load through the Load file dialog (rail key ``l``).
                await pilot.press("l")
                for _ in range(6):
                    await pilot.pause()
                assert await pilot.click("#load_path"), "click missed #load_path"
                await pilot.press(*str(image))
                assert await pilot.click("#load_ok"), "click missed #load_ok"
                await _poll(
                    pilot, lambda: "Loaded firmware.s19" in _painted_status(app)
                )
                work_copy = tmp_path / ".s19tool" / "workarea" / "temp" / "firmware.s19"
                outcomes["pre_save_bytes"] = work_copy.read_bytes()
                # 2. Patch Editor (rail key ``6``): one entry at 0x2000.
                await pilot.press("6")
                for _ in range(6):
                    await pilot.pause()
                assert await pilot.click("#patch_entry_address_input"), (
                    "click missed #patch_entry_address_input"
                )
                await pilot.press(*"0x2000")
                assert await pilot.click("#patch_entry_bytes_input"), (
                    "click missed #patch_entry_bytes_input"
                )
                await pilot.press(*"AA")
                assert await pilot.click("#patch_entry_add_button"), (
                    "click missed #patch_entry_add_button"
                )
                await _poll(pilot, lambda: "entry added" in _painted_status(app))
                assert await pilot.click("#patch_doc_apply_button"), (
                    "click missed #patch_doc_apply_button"
                )
                await _poll(pilot, lambda: "Apply:" in _painted_status(app))
                # 3. Save-back under the loaded image's own name: the prompt
                #    renders in the editor's JSON pane � pick that tab by
                #    clicking its chip, then wait for the prompt to be PAINTED.
                assert await pilot.click("#patch_tab_json"), (
                    "click missed #patch_tab_json"
                )
                await _poll(pilot, lambda: "patched" in _painted_text(app))
                assert await pilot.click("#patch_saveback_name_input"), (
                    "click missed #patch_saveback_name_input"
                )
                await pilot.press("ctrl+shift+a")
                await pilot.press(*"firmware.s19")
                assert await pilot.click("#patch_saveback_confirm_button"), (
                    "click missed #patch_saveback_confirm_button"
                )
                await _poll(
                    pilot,
                    lambda: (
                        tmp_path / ".s19tool" / "workarea" / "firmware.s19"
                    ).is_file(),
                )
                outcomes["work_copy_exists"] = work_copy.is_file()
                outcomes["work_copy_bytes"] = (
                    work_copy.read_bytes() if work_copy.is_file() else None
                )
                # 4. Save project through the real dialog (key ``s``).
                await pilot.press("s")
                for _ in range(6):
                    await pilot.pause()
                # Inc 11 re-pin (census B, LLR-175.2 / .4b): no parent field;
                # the summary of ``proj`` is painted before Save.
                assert await pilot.click("#project_name"), "click missed #project_name"
                await pilot.press(*"proj")
                await sd.wait_for(
                    pilot, lambda: "proj" in sd.where_text(app.screen), "the summary of proj"
                )
                for _ in range(4):
                    await pilot.pause()
                assert await pilot.click("#save_ok"), "click missed #save_ok"
                # the manifest line paints FIRST and the save's success �
                # "Saved project to � (variant 'firmware')" � is the NEWEST
                # line (batch-100 Inc 2 fix pass 1, F1 / LED .52(b): a save
                # that can still fail must not have said "Saved"), so the
                # success line survives as the newest line. Wait for the final
                # line, then read the PAINTED log lines (review finding LOW-2:
                # the wait and the assertion are consistent, and the painted
                # status names the image file).
                await _poll(
                    pilot, lambda: _painted_status(app).startswith("Saved project to")
                )
                outcomes["status"] = _painted_status(app)
                outcomes["log_lines"] = [
                    str(app.query_one(f"#log_line_{i}", Label).render())
                    for i in (1, 2, 3, 4)
                ]
                outcomes["app_running"] = bool(
                    app.query_one("#status_text", Label).render()
                )
            return outcomes

mem_map = {4096: 160, 4097: 161, 4098: 162, 4099: 163, ...}
rows = [SegRow(name='xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx...xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx', kind='a2l', base=4096, size=8)]

    def write_split_join_export(
        mem_map: Dict[int, int],
        rows: List[SegRow],
        *,
        mode: str,
        gap: str,
        pad_byte: int,
        fmt: str,
        stem: str,
        dest_dir: Path,
        zero_coverage_empty: bool = False,
        declared_ranges: Optional[Sequence[Tuple[int, int]]] = None,
        workarea_root: Optional[Path] = None,
    ) -> List[PlacedExport]:
        """Write the planned export (LLR-136) � the engine does the bytes.

        Split: one file per row via :func:`segment_ops.fill_range` (``intra_gap``
        policy inside the file's own span). Join: one ``�stem�_joined.�ext>`` via
        :func:`segment_ops.join_concat` / :func:`segment_ops.join_fill` � the
        latter raises :class:`GapConflictError` BEFORE a byte is emitted when a
        pad would clobber source (the fixed ``on_gap_conflict="abort"`` law), so
        a refused export writes nothing. S19/HEX ride the shipped mem_map
        emitters through :func:`segment_ops.emit_image`; BIN is the flat bytes
        plus the one-line ``.addr`` sidecar (LLR-137.2).

        Every name is built by :func:`build_export_basename` (LLR-160.1) and
        every file is PLACED � single files through
        :func:`workspace.place_into_workarea`, a BIN and its ``.addr`` sidecar as
        one group through :func:`workspace.place_group_into_workarea`
        (B99-I2a, LLR-160.4): a changed re-export takes the next free ``_<N>``,
        an identical one is reused, and each returned :class:`PlacedExport`
        carries the action (``placed.action in {"wrote", "reused"}``) for the
        status (UX-C11) � the word comes only from the placement's
        ``PlacementResult`` (LLR-163.6).

        Before any folder or file is created the destination home passes the
        one shared workspace destination guard ``_guarded_destination`` with
        ``dest_dir`` UNRESOLVED (LLR-160.2, LLR-171.4 as amended by LED .49) �
        the work-area root is read off the textual path, so a junction home is
        refused instead of resolved away � each final path resolves inside it,
        and a link or reparse point at a final name refuses the whole export. The
        plan-time pass (LLR-160.5) then checks every path length the bytes will
        travel � each final name with the worst-case ``_<N>`` suffix the
        basename cap reserves room for, and each private staging path, which is
        longer than the final path � against the platform limit, and every
        final name (sidecars included, case-folded) for collisions. The whole
        export refuses, naming the colliding segments (sanitised, each raw name
        through ``safe_text`` clipped to 80) or the refused name, before any
        byte. When a write still fails part-way the raised
        :class:`ExportWriteError` reports what already happened, split
        truthfully between written and reused files.

        ``zero_coverage_empty`` (the UI's LLR-136.6 limb, default off): a split
        row whose span holds no source bytes exports as the EMPTY image at its
        base (0-byte BIN + sidecar / header-only S19/HEX) instead of the
        intra_gap fill's fabricated all-pad run. Off, this function is pure
        engine composition (LLR-137.3's fill materializes every pad).

        ``declared_ranges`` (LLR-160.3, optional): the loaded image's declared
        ranges; when given, the bytes come ONLY from those ranges � a MAC-only
        session's placeholder entries are never data, so its export is
        header-only. ``None`` keeps the legacy whole-map behaviour (the engine
        purity contract the shipped tests pin).

        ``workarea_root`` (optional): the explicit work-area root this app's
        caller passes (B100-I14, security finding SEC-1, LED .50) � threaded to
        the guard and both placements so "this work area" is THIS app's work
        area, never one the destination's own text names. ``None`` derives the
        root from ``dest_dir``'s textual path (the pre-LED-.50 behaviour).
        """
        EXPORT_FORMATS = _engine_attr("EXPORT_FORMATS")
        emit_image = _engine_attr("emit_image")
        fill_range = _engine_attr("fill_range")
        join_concat = _engine_attr("join_concat")
        join_fill = _engine_attr("join_fill")
        emit_bin_from_mem_map = _engine_attr("emit_bin_from_mem_map")
        bin_addr_sidecar = _engine_attr("bin_addr_sidecar")
        if fmt not in EXPORT_FORMATS:
            raise ValueError(f"fmt must be one of {list(EXPORT_FORMATS)}, got {fmt!r}")
        dest_dir = Path(dest_dir)
        if declared_ranges is not None:
            mem_map = _filter_declared_ranges(mem_map, declared_ranges)

        # --- Phase A: the plan � every final name and every payload BEFORE any
        # byte is written (LLR-160.5). The engine's abort law still lands here,
        # before the placement, so a refused export writes nothing.
        limbs: List[Tuple[str, str, List[Tuple[str, object]]]] = []

        def _plan_limb(
            raw: str, index: int, planned_map, planned_ranges
        ) -> Tuple[str, str, List[Tuple[str, object]]]:
            part = sanitize_export_segment(raw, index)
            if fmt == "bin":
                base = build_export_basename(stem, raw, ".addr", index)
                members = [
                    (
                        f"{base}.bin",
                        emit_bin_from_mem_map(planned_map, planned_ranges),
                    ),
                    (
                        f"{base}.addr",
                        bin_addr_sidecar(planned_ranges[0][0] if planned_ranges else 0),
                    ),
                ]
                return (raw, part, members)
            payload = emit_image(planned_map, planned_ranges, fmt)
            base = build_export_basename(stem, raw, f".{fmt}", index)
            return (raw, part, [(f"{base}.{fmt}", payload)])

        if mode == "split":
            for index, r in enumerate(rows):
                if zero_coverage_empty and not any(
                    r.base <= a < r.base + r.size for a in mem_map
                ):
                    # The UI's LLR-136.6 limb: a confirmed zero-coverage file
                    # exports AT its base as the empty image � BIN 0 bytes + the
                    # .addr sidecar, S19/HEX header-only (the emitters' shipped
                    # semantics) � never a fabricated all-pad run. The default
                    # False keeps this module pure engine composition (the
                    # intra_gap fill materializes pads, LLR-137.3).
                    planned_map, planned_ranges = {}, [(r.base, r.base)]
                else:
                    planned_map, planned_ranges = fill_range(
                        mem_map, r.base, r.base + r.size, pad_byte, intra_gap=gap
                    )
                limbs.append(_plan_limb(r.name, index, planned_map, planned_ranges))
        else:
            ranges = [
                (r.base, r.base + r.size) for r in sorted(rows, key=lambda r: r.base)
            ]
            if gap == "concat":
                planned_map, planned_ranges = join_concat(mem_map, ranges)
            else:
                planned_map, planned_ranges = join_fill(mem_map, ranges, pad_byte)
            limbs.append(_plan_limb("joined", 0, planned_map, planned_ranges))

        # --- LLR-160.2 / LLR-171.4 (LED .49, review finding F1; LED .50,
        # SEC-1): the destination home passes the ONE shared workspace guard
        # BEFORE any folder or file is created, with dest_dir UNRESOLVED � the
        # guard reads the work-area root off the textual path (or the explicit
        # root the caller passes), so a junction home is caught here instead of
        # being resolved away.  The guard returns the RESOLVED pair; the staging
        # paths below ride the resolved root, while the placements in Phase B
        # re-run the guard on the same unresolved dest_dir with the CALLER'S
        # root kept distinct (review finding R3-1): rebinding the placements'
        # root to the resolved root made every export refuse when the app's
        # base folder is itself reached through a junction � the unresolved
        # destination is not textually under the resolved root, so the guard's
        # walk climbed past it to the drive root and refused on the base
        # folder's own junction.
        home, resolved_root = _guarded_destination(dest_dir, workarea_root)

        # Resolve every final path inside the home; refuse a link or reparse
        # point already sitting at a final name (sidecars included) � the write
        # never goes through it.
        final_names: List[Tuple[str, str, str]] = []  # (raw segment, part, name)
        for raw, part, members in limbs:
            for name, _payload in members:
                if _is_reparse_point(home / name):
                    raise ExportRefusedError(
                        f'"{name}" already exists as a link or reparse point � '
                        "refusing to write through it",
                        [(raw, part)],
                    )
                final_names.append((raw, part, name))

        # --- LLR-160.5: the full-path length pass, then the case-folded
        # collision pass � each refuses the WHOLE export, naming the refused
        # name or the colliding segments, BEFORE any byte is written. The
        # length pass measures every path the bytes will travel: the final
        # name carrying the worst-case ``_<N>`` suffix the basename cap
        # reserves room for (the placement seam may claim ``_<N>``), and the
        # private staging path, which is longer than the final path.
        # ``_private_stage_dir`` names its per-write directory ``stage-`` plus
        # the eight random characters ``tempfile.mkdtemp`` appends � the worst
        # case is measured so a path that only fits unstaged is refused here.
        stage_worst = (
            resolved_root
            / WORKAREA_TEMP
            / WORKAREA_STAGING_DIRNAME
            / ("stage-" + "9" * 8)
        )
        for raw, part, name in final_names:
            suffix = Path(name).suffix
            stem_part = name[: -len(suffix)] if suffix else name
            worst_name = (
                f"{stem_part}_{'9' * _EXPORT_SUFFIX_DIGITS}{suffix}"
                if suffix
                else f"{name}_{'9' * _EXPORT_SUFFIX_DIGITS}"
            )
            full = str(home / worst_name)
            if len(full) > _MAX_FULL_PATH:
                raise ExportRefusedError(
                    f'"{name}" resolves to {len(full)} characters � over the '
                    f"platform limit of {_MAX_FULL_PATH}",
                    [(raw, part)],
                )
            staged_full = str(stage_worst / worst_name)
            if len(staged_full) > _MAX_FULL_PATH:
>               raise ExportRefusedError(
                    f'"{name}" stages at {len(staged_full)} characters � over '
                    f"the platform limit of {_MAX_FULL_PATH}",
                    [(raw, part)],
                )
E               s19_app.tui.segment_split_view.ExportRefusedError: "t_xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx.s19" stages at 263 characters � over the platform limit of 260

    def test_at_b99_11_sanitised_mac_only_second_export_takes_suffix(

tmp_path = WindowsPath('[USER_HOME]/Github/s19_app-b103-g3-repro-residual/head/main/tmp/test_at_b100_18_worker_half_ca0')

    def test_at_b100_18_worker_half_case06_project_load_by_keys(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
        """AT-B100-18 worker half, HLR-177 (P1-84): the engineer presses ``p``,
        moves to the ``P6`` row (case_06 image, MAC and 37.7 MB A2L) and
        presses ``enter`` \u2192 while the load runs, ``#status_text`` shows the
        progress line and its elapsed time changes at least once; when it ends
        the status log's last line starts ``Loaded project 'P6' ->
        firmware.s19 + firmware.mac - variant 1 of 1`` and ``#status_text``
        reads ``Loaded project 'P6' -> firmware.s19 + firmware.mac``.

        Why: an elapsed time that moves is what tells the engineer the app is
        working, not frozen. Negative control (P12b-5, executed): the load
        blocks the loop for 5.81 s \u2192 no progress line is ever sampled \u2192 RED."""
        base = tmp_path / "app"
        trio(W(base) / "P6", CASE06)
        pattern = re.compile(r"^[" + FRAMES + r"] Loading project P6  �  step [1-4]/4 .+  �  (\d\d:\d\d)$")

        async def scenario(app, pilot, log):
            await ld.open_project_through_dialog(pilot, app, "P6", confirm="enter", wait_closed=False)
            elapsed = []
            deadline = time.monotonic() + 300
            while True:
                text = status(app)
                m = pattern.match(text)
                if m:
                    elapsed.append(m.group(1))
                elif elapsed or text.startswith("Loaded project"):
                    break
                if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                    raise AssertionError("the P6 load never ended")
                await pilot.pause(0.05)
            await wait(pilot, lambda: status(app).startswith("Loaded project"), "the end of the P6 load", 300)
            for _ in range(4):
                await pilot.pause()
            return elapsed, painted_log(app)[-1], status(app)

>       elapsed, last, final = run(base, scenario)

tmp_path = WindowsPath('[USER_HOME]/Github/s19_app-b103-g3-repro-residual/head/main/tmp/test_tc_b100_08_reset_runs_onc0')

    def test_tc_b100_08_reset_runs_once_before_the_first_install(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
        """One call per project load, ordered before the first install; P1's
        two installs (S19 then MAC) both follow it. A load of the SAME project
        again is a load-over � still exactly one reset."""

        async def _drive() -> list[str]:
            app = S19TuiApp(base_dir=tmp_path)
            _seed_project_p1(app.workarea)
            events: list[str] = []
            original_reset = getattr(app, "_reset_project_session", None)
            # Inc 7 re-pin (LLR-169.6): the project variant now installs through
            # ``_install_project_image`` (parse first, then apply), the MAC
            # through ``load_selected_file``; both apply through
            # ``_apply_loaded_file``, so that is the install marker.
            original_apply = app._apply_loaded_file

            def _recording_reset() -> int:
                events.append("reset")
                if original_reset is None:
                    return 0
                return original_reset()

            def _recording_apply(loaded, path, load_started) -> None:
                events.append("install")
                original_apply(loaded, path, load_started)

            app._reset_project_session = _recording_reset
            app._apply_loaded_file = _recording_apply
            async with app.run_test() as pilot:
                await pilot.pause()
                app._handle_load_project(P1_NAME)
                await _flush(pilot)
                app._handle_load_project(P1_NAME)
                await _flush(pilot)
            return events

        events = asyncio.run(_drive())
        assert events.count("reset") == 2, events
        assert events[0] == "reset", events
>       assert events.index("reset") < events.index("install"), events
E       ValueError: 'install' is not in list

    async def wait_until(
        pilot,
        predicate: Callable[[], bool],
        failure: Union[str, Callable[[], str]],
        *,
        timeout: float = WAIT_TIMEOUT_S,
        pause_first: bool = False,
    ) -> None:
        """Poll ``predicate`` until it is truthy, yielding to the app between
        checks; raise ``AssertionError`` with ``failure`` at the deadline.

        A callable ``failure`` is called only when this raises, so its text can
        show state accumulated up to the timeout.
        """
        deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout
        if pause_first:
            await pilot.pause()
        while True:
            if predicate():
                return
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
>               raise AssertionError(failure() if callable(failure) else failure)

___________________ test_no_textual_in_static_import_graph ____________________

    def test_no_textual_in_static_import_graph() -> None:
        """The check engine and the change service reach no Textual import.

        Intent: LLR-004.4 / F-Q-07 � the no-Textual guarantee verified by
        static import-graph inspection: every ``s19_app``-internal source file
        transitively reachable from ``changes/check.py`` and
        ``services/change_service.py`` imports neither ``textual`` nor any
        ``textual.*`` submodule. The subprocess dotted-import alternative is
        deliberately NOT used here: importing ``s19_app.tui.changes.check``
        executes the parent ``s19_app/tui/__init__.py``, which legitimately
        imports the Textual app for the ``s19tui`` entry point � a packaging
        artifact, not a dependency of the engine's code. The static graph is
        the LLR's named alternative and inspects the code-level dependencies
        themselves (and an in-session ``sys.modules`` assertion stays banned �
        F-Q-07).
        """
        roots = [
            "s19_app.tui.changes.check",
            "s19_app.tui.services.change_service",
        ]
        pending = list(roots)
        visited: set[str] = set()
        offenders: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
        while pending:
            module_name = pending.pop()
            if module_name in visited:
                continue
            visited.add(module_name)
            path = _module_file(module_name)
            if path is None:
                continue
            for imported in sorted(_imported_names(path, module_name)):
                if imported == "textual" or imported.startswith("textual."):
                    offenders.append((module_name, imported))
                elif imported not in visited:
                    pending.append(imported)
        assert "s19_app.tui.changes.check" in visited
        assert "s19_app.tui.services.change_service" in visited
        assert len(visited) > 10, "graph walk must actually traverse the tree"
>       assert offenders == [], (
            f"textual reached the headless check path: {offenders}"
        )

tmp_path = WindowsPath('[USER_HOME]/Github/s19_app-b103-g3-repro-residual/head/main/tmp/test_tc136_post_axis_pass_orde0')

    def test_tc136_post_axis_pass_ordering_and_precedence(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
        """The summer runs AFTER axis_meta is built, and never overrides a non-None length.

        Intent: R2 ordering � ``_inline_axis_counts`` needs the populated
        ``axis_meta`` (built late in the walk), so a CURVE with NO AXIS_DESCR stays
        None (nothing to sum) while one WITH an inline axis derives; and an explicit
        ``LENGTH`` keeps precedence (the ``length is None`` guard).
        """
        layout = ["NO_AXIS_PTS_X 1 UBYTE", "AXIS_PTS_X 2 UBYTE", "FNC_VALUES 3 SWORD"]
        # inline axis present \u2192 derives (1 + 4\xd71 + 4\xd72 = 13)
        derived = _write_a2l(
            tmp_path, cname="C_D", char_type="CURVE", deposit="RL_D",
            layout_lines=layout, axes=[("STD_AXIS", 4)], name="d.a2l",
        )
        assert derived["length"] == 13
        # no AXIS_DESCR \u2192 axis_meta empty \u2192 nothing to sum \u2192 stays None
        no_axis = _write_a2l(
            tmp_path, cname="C_NA", char_type="CURVE", deposit="RL_NA",
            layout_lines=layout, axes=[], name="na.a2l",
        )
        assert no_axis["axis_meta"] == []
        assert no_axis["length"] is None
        # explicit LENGTH keeps precedence over the summer
        explicit = _write_a2l(
            tmp_path, cname="C_L", char_type="CURVE", deposit="RL_L",
            layout_lines=layout, axes=[("STD_AXIS", 4)],
            extra_char_lines="      LENGTH 999\n", name="l.a2l",
        )
>       assert explicit["length"] == 999

    def test_at_b94_03_expanded_editor_primary_surface(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
        """AT-B94-03 � table + expanded editor visible at 120x30; reachable at 80x24.

        Intent (HLR-003): at 120x30 (boxed workspace 96x18) the entries table
        and the expanded JSON editor are both visible without scroll; at 80x24
        (boxed 74x12) both are reachable by panel scroll. Geometry is measured,
        not assumed (C-23/C-29): the A1 inner-split assumption's verification.
        """

        async def _drive(size) -> dict:
            app = S19TuiApp(base_dir=tmp_path)
            async with app.run_test(size=size) as pilot:
                await pilot.pause()
                await pilot.press("6")
                await pilot.pause()
                service = app._change_service
                service.add_entry("0x100", "", "DE AD BE EF")
                service.add_entry("0x200", "", "DE AD BE EF")
                panel = app.query_one("#patch_editor_panel", PatchEditorPanel)
                panel.refresh_entries(service.rows(None), mem_map=None)
                await pilot.pause()
                # batch-97 Inc-4 (named supersession, HLR-144): the editor is the
                # paste buffer on the inspector's JSON tab, beside the ledger.
                app.query_one("#patch_tab_json").press()
                await pilot.pause()
                table = app.query_one("#patch_doc_entries_table", DataTable)
                editor = app.query_one("#patch_paste_text", TextArea)
                workspace = app.query_one("#workspace_body").region
                visible = {
                    "table_visible": table.region.y >= workspace.y
                    and table.region.y + table.region.height
                    <= workspace.y + workspace.height,
                    "editor_visible": editor.region.y < workspace.y + workspace.height,
                }
                if size == (80, 24):
                    table.scroll_visible()
                    await pilot.pause()
                    visible["table_reachable"] = (
                        table.region.y < workspace.y + workspace.height
                    )
                    editor.scroll_visible()
                    await pilot.pause()
                    visible["editor_reachable"] = (
                        editor.region.y < app.screen.region.height
                    )
                return visible

        at_120 = asyncio.run(_drive((120, 30)))

    def test_ac3_fill_does_not_leak_to_sibling_windows() -> None:
        """Each tab's scroll body fills ITS tab; the JSON fill stays JSON-scoped.

        batch-97 Inc-4 (named supersession, HLR-144 / design �3): the three windows
        became the ledger and two tabs of one inspector. By design every body now
        fills its own region � the ledger body (the hero), the JSON body (the paste
        buffer's share of the JSON tab) and the Checks body (the Checks tab's scroll
        view). What this arm keeps from the batch-48 C-30 intent: the JSON body
        never claims the read-only view's share (it ends where the tab's docked
        rows begin, below the view), and no Patch rule leaks to another screen
        (the scope arm of TC-B97-04, ``tests/test_b97_patch_editor.py``).
        """

        async def _run() -> dict[str, object]:
            app = S19TuiApp()
            async with app.run_test(size=_FILL_TALL) as pilot:
                await _show_patch_json(app, pilot)
                bodies = _json_window_bodies(app)
                view = app.query_one("#patch_json_view", TextArea)
                controls = app.query_one("#patch_paste_controls")
                out = {
                    "json": (bodies["patch_win_json"].region, view.region, controls.region),
                    "script": (
                        app.query_one("#patch_win_script").content_region.height,
                        bodies["patch_win_script"].region.height,
                    ),
                }
                app.query_one("#patch_tab_checks", Button).press()
                await pilot.pause()
                out["checks"] = (
                    app.query_one("#patch_win_checks").content_region.height,
                    bodies["patch_win_checks"].region.height,
                    app.query_one("#patch_checks_controls").region.height,
                )
                return out

        dims = asyncio.run(_run())
        json_body, view, controls = dims["json"]
        assert json_body.y >= view.bottom, (

            f"body {json_body} vs view {view}"

        assert json_body.bottom <= controls.y, (
            f"the JSON body must end where the docked paste controls begin: "
            f"body {json_body} vs controls {controls}"
